## Grand Prix Shizuoka 2013

![](https://i.ormos.uk/64/9d/649d9fa016004a27853abc2ff5818f0ae55224daba9b82a1254f15182181f245.jpg)

Armed with only a single bye, Ryo Nakada fought his way through nearly 1800 players to emerge a champion. Playing a tweaked version of the White Weenie deck that put Ben Stark in the Top 4 of Grand Prix Dallas he swept Day 1 with a perfect 9-0 record, and didn't take a loss until Round 12. His opponent? Fellow finalist Shota Takao. Takao had been turning heads all weekend with his Esper Humans deck, and led the field into the Top 8.

Standard looked to be quite healthy as a variety of decks were on display. Mono-Blue Devotion, Mono-Red and Boros devotion decks, Control decks both Esper and Blue-White, and Black-based midrange decks, whether mono-colored or Black-White. That diversity was on display in the Top 8 as well. As the quarterfinals began, eyes were on the two big names, 2011 World Champion Junya Iyanaga and former Player of the Year Shouta Yasooka. Both of them fell to Takao's Esper Humans. Meanwhile on the other side of the bracket, Nakada swept Keisuke Sato's Colossal Gruul deck and scored a thrilling last-second victory against Hajime Nakashima's Mono-Blue Devotion.

It was only fitting that Nakada and Takao's match would go to three games. The hall was oddly quiet as spectators crowded around the feature match area three deep, and more watched rapt at big screens showing the live video coverage. In the decider, Nakada curved out an army, protected from [Supreme Verdict](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Supreme+Verdict) by [Xathrid Necromancer](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Xathrid+Necromancer), and overran Takao.

Congratulations to Ryo Nakada, Grand Prix Shizuoka Champion!

[Grand Prix Shizuoka video coverage](http://live.nicovideo.jp/watch/lv163148849) provided by NicoNico Live.

## Sunday, 2:00 p.m. – Deck Tech: Shota Takao's Esper Humans

*by Ben Swartz*

 

Finishing 4-0 in a **Magic Online** daily event one week before, Shota Takao decided to bring his Esper Midrange deck here to Shizuoka. It has proved well for him so far, escorting him to a dominating 13-0 record.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/36/02/36028f15a95c883968f4feb61819a68d450a0b393e1fd0378edb952ac78abbd6.jpg)  
*Shota Takao*

Takao was fascinated by Paul Rietzl's deck from Pro Tour *Theros*. [Xathrid Necromancer](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Xathrid+Necromancer) paired with other humans formed the start of the deck for the Tokyo player. After losing repeatedly to [Pack Rat](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Pack+Rat) he thought, "what if I were to add [Detention Sphere](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Detention+Sphere)?" By adding blue he also gained access to [Lyev Skyknight](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lyev+Skyknight).

![Lyev Skyknight](http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&name=Lyev+Skyknight)

Takao explained that adding a third color did not make his mana too much worse. He was able to take advantage of [Hallowed Fountain](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Hallowed+Fountain), [Watery Grave](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Watery+Grave), and [Godless Shrine](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Godless+Shrine), along with [Temple of Deceit](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Temple+of+Deceit) and [Temple of Silence](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Temple+of+Silence). Since he had [Detention Sphere](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Detention+Sphere), he could easily cut Hero's Downfall--a card that would have only made his mana worse.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/20/07/2007275d0cad551cfd863b16f30589e5a6c79d89a9512f4db6c044c9b05b9a22.jpg)  
*Shota Takao explaining his his 3rd color decision.*

Takao believes that he has a strong matchup against the three major decks in the format: Mono-blue Devotion, Mono-black Devotion, and blue-white control. In those matchups, he explained that [Whip of Erebos](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Whip+of+Erebos) and [Obzedat, Ghost Council](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Obzedat%2C+Ghost+Council) were especially important.

In building the deck, Takao had some interesting choices. For two-drops he was unsure which of [Imposing Sovereign](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Imposing+Sovereign), [Cartel Aristocrat](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cartel+Aristocrat), or [Daring Skyjek](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Daring+Skyjek) was best. He expected some amount of [Domestication](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Domestication)s this weekend, against which [Cartel Aristocrat](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cartel+Aristocrat) is especially strong.

Similarly, he had a hard time deciding which removal spell would be best, hence the split of one [Doom Blade](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Doom+Blade), one [Ultimate Price](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ultimate+Price) and one [Far & Away](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Far+%2F%2F+Away).

![](https://i.ormos.uk/63/9f/639f291b08ab621e7b2516634cd1d6882beeb9e43d60d51838dbaa33f20b755d.jpg)  
*Shota Takao*

Takao reiterated the importance of [Obzedat, Ghost Council](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Obzedat%2C+Ghost+Council). "While [Blood Baron of Vizkopa](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?&name=Blood%2BBaron%2Bof%2BVizkopa) of Viskopa is by no means weak, Obzedat is on a different level." With Obzedat missing from top tables as of late, Takao hoped that this tournament would spring it back into the minds of players.

![Obzedat, Ghost Council](http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&name=Obzedat%2C+Ghost+Council)

Being a lock for top eight here at Grand Prix Shizuoka, Takao had his eyes on the prize. "I really want to win this event." He hopes to follow up this tournament with a solid finish at Pro Tour *Journey into Nyx*.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/fb/6e/fb6eb682caf2f2a779ba107a68deb52e4cd09d559517a30b3d2e89e3b9ed1572.jpg)

### Shōta Takao

**Age:** 24

**Residence:**  
 Tōkyō

**Occupation:**  
 company employee  
**Notable **Magic** Achievements**: Japan Nationals Top 8, 10th place at Grand Prix Nagoya

**Where do you normally play **Magic**?**  
 Hareru-ya

**What deck did you play this weekend?**  
 Esper Mid-range

**Why did you choose this deck?**  
 It can just win.  
 Swiss Record- Byes: 0, Day 1: 9-0, Day 2: 5-1

**This year is the 20th anniversary of **Magic**. Of those, what year stands out most in your memory?**  
 2009—it was a year I did best in tournaments.

**What is your favorite card in Standard?**  
[Obzedat, Ghost Council](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Obzedat%2C+Ghost+Council)

![](https://i.ormos.uk/c5/b9/c5b9a2b6fe1d6ab7e429291d9fe7343935466a6686699a04aa1ba34c3e0ef964.jpg)

 

 

### Hajime Nakashima

**Age:** 24

**Residence:**  
 Tōkyō

**Occupation:**  
 student  
**Notable **Magic** Achievements**: Top 16, Grand Prix Nagoya 2012; Top 64, Grand Prix Yokohama 2013

**Where do you normally play **Magic**?**  
 Kateru-ya, Hareru-ya, PWC

**What deck did you play this weekend?**  
 Mono-blue

**Why did you choose this deck?**  
 Because it's easy to play.  
 Swiss Record- Byes: 2, Day 1: 8-1, Day 2: 5-0-1

**This year is the 20th anniversary of **Magic**. Of those, what year stands out most in your memory?**  
 Around the time of Masques and *Invasion*—that was my first Standard environment.

**What is your favorite card in Standard?**  
[Thassa](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thassa%2C+God+of+the+Sea)!

![](https://i.ormos.uk/84/89/8489aeefd26f68d02b1f97b4b055e887207d38ce982f5e60e97e5a85d2bdef6a.jpg)

 student

*Lorwyn*. That was the first time I went 3-0 in FNM.

**What is your favorite card in Standard?**  
[Boros Elite](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Boros+Elite).

![](https://i.ormos.uk/af/3a/af3a360a16110d375db084bedb2fa11c7eac8639a0615e3ae81febb87b1be1e5.jpg)

 Home

 Mono-blue

 Next year, when I win the Pro Tour.

 Tōkyō

**Occupation:**

### Keisuke Satō

**Age:** 24

**Residence:**  
 Niigata-ken

**Occupation:**  
 student  
**Notable **Magic** Achievements**: None

**Where do you normally play **Magic**?**  
 Cards of Paradise in Niigata, Game-an Beat, Top Boy, Duel Gard

 Gruul Mid-range

**Why did you choose this deck?**  
 I've been using it since it was first playable in the environment, and I like RG.

**This year is the 20th anniversary of **Magic**. Of those, what year stands out most in your memory?**  
 Around Masques/*Invasion*. That was a fun environment. I loved playing with [Fires of Yavimaya](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Fires+of+Yavimaya).

**What is your favorite card in Standard?**  
 Either [Stormbreath Dragon](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Stormbreath+Dragon) or [Ghor-Clan Rampager](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ghor-Clan+Rampager).

 company employee

 Swiss Record- Byes: 2, Day 1: 7-2, Day 2: 6-0

**This year is the 20th anniversary of **Magic**. Of those, what year stands out most in your memory?**  
 2009, the year I first became a pro player.

**What is your favorite card in Standard?**  
 Sphinx's Revelation—if you can get it off, it always gives you something you can use.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/35/c2/35c2624323bb48e8ae82ef47062e07a890e81bb95b2c7d9117a861a9343d24a0.jpg)

 

 

### Jun'ya Iyanaga

**Age:** 26

**Residence:**  
 Tōkyō

**Occupation:**  
**Notable **Magic** Achievements**: World Champion 2011

**Where do you normally play **Magic**?**  
 Nowhere

**What deck did you play this weekend?**  
 Esper

**Why did you choose this deck?**  
 I was planning on use the Kateru-ya mono-black deck, but I wasn't able to find enough [Mutavault](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mutavault)s to put it together.  
 Swiss Record- Byes: 1, Day 1: 6-2, Day 2: 6-0

**This year is the 20th anniversary of **Magic**. Of those, what year stands out most in your memory?**  
 2005. That was the year when there were lots of tournaments and tournament players in Japan, and the year I played the most.

**What is your favorite card in Standard?**  
[Notion Thief](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Notion+Thief)

## Quarterfinals - Jun'ya Iyanaga vs. Shota Takao

*by Ben Swartz*

 

After running the tables with a 14-1 record, Shota Takao was ready to play in the top eight. His inventive Esper Midrange deck had caught the attention of players here in Shizuoka as it earned Takao win after win. Based off of the Orzhov deck that Paul Rietzl used at Pro Tour *Theros*, Takao added blue to give him access to [Detention Sphere](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Detention+Sphere).

But he had his work cut out for him; former World Champion, Jun'ya Iyanaga, was his opponent in the quarterfinals. Finishing the swiss in 8th place, Iyanaga had barely made it in; armed with his Esper Control deck he hoped to teach Takao a lesson.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/62/c4/62c4c780bd6c15fd8109264b5dede946912c0faea622c85b4f2ba1ff3ad984ec.jpg)  
*Jun'ya Iyanaga vs. Shota Takao* 

When interviewed about his deck, Takao mentioned that he believed he had an excellent matchup against control decks, including Iyanaga's Esper control deck. With [Soldier of the Pantheon](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Soldier+of+the+Pantheon) being immune to [Detention Sphere](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Detention+Sphere) and [Xathrid Necromancer](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Xathrid+Necromancer) giving pseudo-protection from [Supreme Verdict](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Supreme+Verdict), Takao's deck seemed favored if he could get a quick start.

### The Games

Game one ended in a flash. Takao played [Soldier of the Pantheon](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Soldier+of+the+Pantheon) on turns one and two. Along with a [Mutavault](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mutavault), the 2/1s continued to knock chunks out of Iyanaga's lifetotal. When Iyanaga was unable to come up with a [Supreme Verdict](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Supreme+Verdict) on the fourth turn, the writing was on the wall. A few turns later, Takao was up a game.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/91/f4/91f4bba4c8470ca0b0e4a0f7d06818b92b8701f4e308646c1c1c4f95ed3d50c2.jpg)  
*Jun'ya Iyanaga* 

### Takao 1 - 0 Iyanaga

Takao's start was not as quick in game two. After mulliganing to 5, all he could muster up was a second turn [Daring Skyjek](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Daring+Skyjek).

After Iyanaga missed his third land drop, Takao decided to cast [Thoughtseize](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thoughtseize). Takao sniped [Detention Sphere](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Detention+Sphere) out of Iyanaga's hand, leaving him without an immediate answer to [Daring Skyjek](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Daring+Skyjek). Nabbing a [Supreme Verdict](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Supreme+Verdict) with a second [Thoughtseize](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thoughtseize) the following turn and casting a Lyev Sknight, the already land light Iyanaga floundered. Without an answer, Iyanaga folded to the pair of 3/1s.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/b7/27/b7278015ff234126ad2fca6d03f9423ad68ce18890d53203a619235a665a261e.jpg)  
*Shota Takao* 

### Shota Takao defeats Jun'ya Iyanaga 2-0 and advances to the semifinals.

* * *

## Quarterfinal Roundup

*by Josh Bennett*

 

Here's a quick look at how three of the quarterfinal matches shook out.

### Kazuaki Fujimura (Mono-Blue Devotion) vs. Shouta Yasooka (Blue-Black Control)

The match began on a sour note, as Yasooka was issued a game loss for a deck list error. He accepted the penalty with good grace and they shuffled up for what would be game two.

A turn-one [Thoughtseize](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thoughtseize) for Yasooka gave him the lay of the land, letting him plan out his hand of [Syncopate](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Syncopate) and Removal. Fujimura played out [Cloudfin Raptor](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cloudfin+Raptor) and [Frostburn Weird](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Frostburn+Weird), but couldn't stick anything else before Yasooka summoned [Prognostic Sphinx](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Prognostic+Sphinx). That gave Fujimura a window to resolve Thassa, but soon Yasooka was going one better with [Opportunity](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Opportunity). The card advantage proved too much.

### Fujimura 1 - Yasooka 1

So they would play one game for all the marbles. Fujimura's seven were no good, and unfortunately his six weren't much better. He stayed at five, but at that point Yasooka didn't need much to get well out in front. [Ashiok, Nightmare Weaver](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ashiok%2C+Nightmare+Weaver) went unopposed, and a few turns later Fujimura conceded.

  
*Kazuaki Fujimura (Mono-Blue Devotion) vs. Shouta Yasooka (Blue-Black Control)* 

### Shouta Yasooka defeats Kazuaki Fujimura 2-1

### Hajime Nakashima (Mono-Blue Devotion) vs. Tadaki Tsukagoshi (Blue-White Control)

Nakashima opened with a pair of [Judge's Familiar](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Judge%27s+Familiar)s and got in some quick damage before Tsukagoshi took care of them with [Detention Sphere](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Detention+Sphere). That let Nakashima get out [Thassa, God of the Sea](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thassa%2C+God+of+the+Sea). Tsukagoshi was able to stop the first [Master of Waves](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Master+of+Waves) that followed, but not the second, and then Nakashima had [Nightveil Specter](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Nightveil+Specter) to actualize Thassa. A small [Sphinx's Revelation](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sphinx%27s+Revelation) turned up empty, and soon they were on to game two.

### Nakashima 1 - Tsukagoshi 0

The second game began as a mirror of the first. Nakashima's two Familiars fell to [Detention Sphere](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Detention+Sphere), but then he resolved Thassa. Tsukagoshi had a hand of expensive, powerful spells, and was content to play out lands. He stopped [Nightveil Specter](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Nightveil+Specter) with [Last Breath](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Last+Breath), then dropped [Elspeth, Sun's Champion](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Elspeth%2C+Sun%27s+Champion) and made tokens.

  
*Hajime Nakashima (Mono-Blue Devotion) vs. Tadaki Tsukagoshi (Blue-White Control)* 

Unfortunately for him, Nakashima had a second Specter in hand, and enough mana to make the newly-actual Thassa unblockable, killing Elspeth. They had reached a strained equilibrium, nibbling for damage on either side, but Nakashima had the advantage of Thassa's scry ability to filter his draws. Tsukagoshi matched him card for card, but couldn't find a [Sphinx's Revelation](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sphinx%27s+Revelation) to take over the game. In the end, Thassa served up enough unblockable [Mutavault](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mutavault)s to take the match for Nakashima.

### Hajime Nakashima defeats Tadaki Tsukagoshi 2-0

### Ryo Nakada (White Weenie with Black) vs. Keisuke Sato (Colossal Gruul)

Sato couldn't even get off the blocks in the first game thanks to a backbreaking double mulligan. Nakada's draw was no slouch, two Soldiers of the Pantheon, then [Daring Skyjek](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Daring+Skyjek), then [Banisher Priest](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Banisher+Priest) for [Scavenging Ooze](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Scavenging+Ooze). Sato blunted some of the damage with [Sylvan Caryatid](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sylvan+Caryatid), but Nakada crushed his hopes with [Brave the Elements](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Brave+the+Elements) to force through lethal.

### Nakada 1 - Sato 0

Game two was a much more drawn-out affair. Nakada's draw was a little slow and Sato kept him off creatures in the early game with [Mizzium Mortars](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mizzium+Mortars) and [Shock](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Shock). Xenagos the Reveller diverted some damage while giving Sato a mana boost. His [Polukranos, World Eater](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Polukranos%2C+World+Eater) fell to [Doom Blade](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Doom+Blade), but was replaced by [Ghor-Clan Rampager](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ghor-Clan+Rampager) and his buddy [Domri Rade](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Domri+Rade), who told him to fight a creature, leaving Nakada with just [Precinct Captain](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Precinct+Captain) and [Mutavault](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mutavault) for offence.

It looked bad for Nakada, but his deck had served up two more cheap removal spells, leaving Sato without blockers and costing him both his low-on-loyalty planeswalkers.

  
*Ryo Nakada (White Weenie with Black) vs. Keisuke Sato (Colossal Gruul)* 

Sato refused to go quietly, and his deck agreed, giving him another [Ghor-Clan Rampager](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ghor-Clan+Rampager). Soon it was joined by a pair of [Scavenging Ooze](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Scavenging+Ooze)s. Sato was well-defended, but couldn't do anything to disrupt the army that was amassing across from him. They were supercharged by [Spear of Heliod](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spear+of+Heliod), and then [Xathrid Necromancer](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Xathrid+Necromancer) made attacking an even more attractive prospect. Still, Sato was at a comfortable life total.

Then, [Brave the Elements](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Brave+the+Elements). Not lethal, but enough that Sato was on the back foot. With the Necromancer providing backup troops, he was unable to stop the advancing horde.

### Ryo Nakada defeats Keisuke Sato 2-0

* * *

## Semifinals - Shota Yasooka vs. Shota Takao

*by Ben Swartz*

 

After blasting his way through Jun'ya Iyanaga, Shota Takao faced off against another top Japanese player, Shota Yasooka. Takao had made the claim that his Esper Midrange deck, featuring [Soldier of the Pantheon](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Soldier+of+the+Pantheon), [Xathrid Necromancer](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Xathrid+Necromancer) and [Obzedat, Ghost Council](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Obzedat%2C+Ghost+Council), was favored against control decks.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/83/3b/833bd42d51ada6173aa70b38d8d1586221529635d0ed7c36f1d27cb07def7a39.jpg)  
*Shota Yasooka vs. Shota Takao* 

The question, though, was whether or not it would be favored against Shota Yasooka's blue-black control deck. The former player of the year, came prepared with the same deck that propelled him to a 46th place finish at Pro Tour *Theros*. Though he changed his deck a little bit since his time in Dublin, the premise stayed the same: planeswalkers and removal to control the board and [Prognostic Sphinx](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Prognostic+Sphinx), [Master of Waves](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Master+of+Waves), and [Pack Rat](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Pack+Rat) to finish off his opponent.

Some of the best cards in Yasooka's deck had natural counters in Takao's deck. [Domestication](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Domestication) would be met with [Cartel Aristocrat](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cartel+Aristocrat). [Master of Waves](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Master+of+Waves) and [Pack Rat](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Pack+Rat) would be met with [Detention Sphere](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Detention+Sphere). All this being said, it would be a disservice to count a control deck wielding Shota Yasooka out. Only one Shota could advance to the finals, which would it be?

### The Games

Play began with early armies on both sides: Takao got a [Soldier of the Pantheon](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Soldier+of+the+Pantheon) and a [Lyev Skyknight](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lyev+Skyknight), while Yasooka landed an [Ashiok, Nightmare Weaver](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ashiok%2C+Nightmare+Weaver) and a [Jace, Architect of Thought](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Jace%2C+Architect+of+Thought). A [Detention Sphere](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Detention+Sphere) off the top of Takao's deck took out Jace.

[Domestication](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Domestication) proved its worth, coming down for Yasooka and stealing Takao's [Lyev Skyknight](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lyev+Skyknight). With Yasooka tapped out though, Takao was able to resolve a [Xathrid Necromancer](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Xathrid+Necromancer).

![](https://i.ormos.uk/c5/0c/c50c1135691b8b5dbb27694d1afb270623d48cdf390a48e2d2862581cd6cbee5.jpg)  
*Shota Takao* 

[Xathrid Necromancer](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Xathrid+Necromancer) began doing work: when Yasooka started trading for Takao's creatures, he was rewarded with many Zombie tokens. Yasooka's maindeck [Ratchet Bomb](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ratchet+Bomb)s came in handy, clearing away the zombies while giving him some breathing room to resolve a [Master of Waves](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Master+of+Waves).

A second [Xathrid Necromancer](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Xathrid+Necromancer) came down for Takao. Paired with a [Detention Sphere](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Detention+Sphere) on [Master of Waves](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Master+of+Waves), Yasooka was sent packing for a second game.

### Takao 1 - 0 Yasooka

Both players had a slow start. [Pack Rat](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Pack+Rat) for Yasooka was the first substantial play, which allowed Takao to resolve an [Obzedat, Ghost Council](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Obzedat%2C+Ghost+Council).

![](https://i.ormos.uk/49/96/4996338694bb48b1d7332ceffa1ec4e0d4b0f22261b365f03d6c56a4c0f9c46a.jpg)  
*Shota Yasooka* 

Yasooka attempted to remove the Obzedat with a [Hero's Downfall](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Hero%27s+Downfall), but Takao had a timely [Negate](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Negate) to counter the removal spell. Yasooka tried again on the following turn, this time succeeding.

It would seem as if this would be the time for Yasooka's [Pack Rat](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Pack+Rat) to take over the game, but Takao had an [Underworld Connections](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Underworld+Connections), enabling him to go toe-to-toe with the former player of the year and his rats.

Yasooka put the [Pack Rat](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Pack+Rat) plan on hold, casting a Jace and a [Prognostic Sphinx](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Prognostic+Sphinx). The race began. Jace got eaten and [Pack Rat](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Pack+Rat) began multiplying. With a pair of [Xathrid Necromancer](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Xathrid+Necromancer)s and a [Cartel Aristocrat](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cartel+Aristocrat), a zombie pack amounted.

A [Ratchet Bomb](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ratchet+Bomb) off the top was perfect for Yasooka; it allowed him to blast away the zombie army and buy him time for his [Prognostic Sphinx](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Prognostic+Sphinx).

Takao had a different plan though. He resolved a [Whip of Erebos](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Whip+of+Erebos) and immediately brought back Obzedat. Yasooka barely stayed alive by using [Devour Flesh](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Devour+Flesh) on one of his [Pack Rat](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Pack+Rat)s.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/07/8d/078d4d553445d1372b0197d1ec48eaac981c80bb7f942a93e8ee7eca612b53f8.jpg)  
*Shota Takao's Board* 

Takao continued bringing the beatdown. Thanks to the interaction between [Whip of Erebos](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Whip+of+Erebos) and Obzedat, he got to keep the Ghost Council while returning a [Lyev Skyknight](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lyev+Skyknight) on the following turn.

Yasooka did what he could to try to survive the attack, but Takao's last card in hand was a [Negate](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Negate), countering Yasooka's [Doom Blade](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Doom+Blade) and propelling him to the finals.

### Shota Takao 2 - 0 Shota Yasooka

* * *

## Semifinals - Hajime Nakashima vs. Ryo Nakada

*by Josh Bennett*

 

Both players were eager to get down to the games. They shuffled quickly and fidgeted while they waited for the go-ahead from the Head Judge. It would be Nakashima's Mono-Blue Devotion against the power of Ryo Nakada's White Weenie deck.

Nakashima led with [Cloudfin Raptor](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cloudfin+Raptor) but had nothing to add to the board after Nakada played out [Boros Elite](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Boros+Elite). He took one, and Nakada added second Elite. [Nightveil Specter](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Nightveil+Specter) evolved Nakashima's Raptor and he got in for one. Nakada wasted no time removing the Specter with [Banisher Priest](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Banisher+Priest) and swung in again.

[Tidebinder Mage](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tidebinder+Mage) turned the Raptor into a 2/3, and Nakashima decided it was time to hold back. Nakada added [Imposing Sovereign](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Imposing+Sovereign) and [Precinct Captain](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Precinct+Captain) to his board, preparing to strike. Nakashima summoned Thassa but was still one devotion short. Nakada struck, dropping [Spear of Heliod](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spear+of+Heliod) and attacking with [Precinct Captain](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Precinct+Captain), both [Boros Elite](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Boros+Elite)s and the Sovereign. Nakashima gave him a rude awakening with [Cyclonic Rift](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cyclonic+Rift) on the Spear, letting him trade [Tidebinder Mage](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tidebinder+Mage) for the Sovereign, and eat the Captain for free.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/ef/a8/efa840c356d0f3059ad92dc9c4b3c758ba48b79be40de5e1480e7c7664065c1e.jpg)  
*Ryo Nakada* 

It was a turning point, but Nakashima still needed help to close the deal. He found [Frostburn Weird](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Frostburn+Weird) and played it. Nakada thought for a long while before settling on playing a [Xathrid Necromancer](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Xathrid+Necromancer) and passing. Nakashima kept his top card, but made no play. Nakada dropped a second Necromancer, then attacked with the first one and the two [Boros Elite](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Boros+Elite)s. Nakashima played [Rapid Hybridization](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rapid+Hybridization) on [Banisher Priest](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Banisher+Priest), then blocked with the freed Specter, the animated Thassa and his [Cloudfin Raptor](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cloudfin+Raptor).

He had stopped the attack, but at what cost? The flurry of Necromancer triggers had doubled the size of Nakada's army, and he still had the [Spear of Heliod](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spear+of+Heliod) from earlier in his hand. Nakashima attacked with [Nightveil Specter](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Nightveil+Specter), hoping to steal a solution to his problems. He did not, and scooped up his cards.

### Nakada 1 - Nakashima 0

Nakashima couldn't hide his frustration at drawing up a clunky one-land hand, and immediately mulliganed. His six were unplayable, so he went to five. Nakada started things off with a [Soldier of the Pantheon](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Soldier+of+the+Pantheon) but spent his second turn on a scry land.

If you had to start with a five-card hand, you could do a lot worse than what Nakashima had to work with. He curved out [Frostburn Weird](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Frostburn+Weird) into Thassa, God of the sea. After Nakada played [Imposing Sovereign](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Imposing+Sovereign), Nakashima was free to play [Nightveil Specter](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Nightveil+Specter) and bash in with his animated Thassa. Nakada had [Banisher Priest](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Banisher+Priest), but Nakashima wasn't out of gas just yet. His last two cards were [Cloudfin Raptor](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cloudfin+Raptor) and a second [Nightveil Specter](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Nightveil+Specter). Another hit from Thassa put Nakada to twelve.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/da/bb/dabb7036ef07964768d336e65f079d5741cb92f78c9ad1b016e29bc4fb940e92.jpg)  
*Hajime Nakashima* 

Nakada wasn't done yet. [Doom Blade](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Doom+Blade) took care of [Frostburn Weird](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Frostburn+Weird) and his army waltzed past Nakashima's tapped creatures for six damage, leaving him at eight,. He played out [Precinct Captain](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Precinct+Captain) and passed. Still, the God of the Sea would not be denied, and she sent her [Master of Waves](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Master+of+Waves) to Nakashima's aid. He passed it back, not willing to risk an attack while his elemental army was tapped. Nakada couldn't attack either, and played out another Sovereign and [Xathrid Necromancer](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Xathrid+Necromancer). Nakashima counted up an all-out attack, but it would have been one damage short, and so just hit for seven with an unblockable Thassa and the [Nightveil Specter](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Nightveil+Specter). Nakada crossed his fingers and hoped for [Brave the Elements](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Brave+the+Elements), but it wasn't waiting for him.

### Nakada 1 - Nakashima 1

Yet another mulligan for Nakashima. This time he stopped at six. Nakada came out guns blazing with a pair of Soldiers of the Pantheon, but Nakada was prepared with [Ratchet Bomb](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ratchet+Bomb). Nakada hit for four and added [Imposing Sovereign](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Imposing+Sovereign) to his board.

Nakashima cleared out the Soldiers and summoned a tapped [Frostburn Weird](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Frostburn+Weird). Nakada hit for two and passed, then killed [Frostburn Weird](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Frostburn+Weird) with [Orzhov Charm](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Orzhov+Charm) after Nakashima played Thassa. The Sovereign and a [Mutavault](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mutavault) put Nakashima to eight, but Nakada didn't have another play.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/68/e8/68e816d6bf8d8f23dcc6a77fe6a9f3fd7714a6d57e18eb37a24833ddf284810e.jpg)  
*Hajime Nakashima* 

Nakashima bolstered his defenses with [Jace, Architect of Thought](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Jace%2C+Architect+of+Thought), upping his loyalty. Nakada swung again with his nerfed creatures and left Nakashima at six. He untapped and added a tapped [Tidebinder Mage](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tidebinder+Mage) and a [Mutavault](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mutavault) to his board, then hit for five with Thassa. Nakada hit back with Sovereign and [Mutavault](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mutavault), and stopped Nakashima from blocking with [Mutavault](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mutavault) thanks to [Doom Blade](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Doom+Blade). Nakashima was down to just four life. Nakada added [Precinct Captain](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Precinct+Captain) and passed.

Nakashima's hands shook as he took his next turn, knowing the game was balanced on a knife edge. He had [Tidebinder Mage](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tidebinder+Mage), and Jace's -1/-0 ability, and an animate Thassa. He thought and thought, and decided to play it safe, summoning [Master of Waves](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Master+of+Waves) and passing the turn with all his defenses up.

Would it be enough? Nakada drew and surveyed the board, then struck. [Banisher Priest](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Banisher+Priest) to get rid of [Tidebinder Mage](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tidebinder+Mage), turning off Thassa in the bargain, and then attacking with two creatures. He turned over his last card: [Profit & Loss](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Profit+%2F%2F+Loss), for exactly lethal!

### Ryo Nakada defeats Hajime Nakashima 2-1

* * *

## Finals - Ryo Nakada vs. Shota Takao

*by Ben Swartz*

 

![](https://i.ormos.uk/35/4e/354e079ae2b75abd6a7d6eb8de455d8ddac30f5846d4a27264933bbc54bd35da.jpg)  
*Shota Takao vs. Ryo Nakada* 

It all came down to this. Shota Takao had proved his dominance thus far, with only a single loss all tournament. His Esper Midrange deck had been strong against any deck that was thrown at him. Armed with Obzedat, [Detention Sphere](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Detention+Sphere), [Xathrid Necromancer](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Xathrid+Necromancer), could Takao be stopped?

One man stood in the way: Ryo Nakada. Quietly, the 23 year old student rose to the top of the standings. Armed with a black-white Humans deck similar to the one Ben Stark played at Grand Prix Dallas/Fort Worth, he had attacked his way through the swiss and eventually to the finals. The potential start of [Soldier of the Pantheon](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Soldier+of+the+Pantheon) into [Precinct Captain](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Precinct+Captain) into [Xathrid Necromancer](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Xathrid+Necromancer) would make any player shake in his or her boots.

The finals would be a classic battle of aggro versus midrange. Takao had some powerful late drops: Obzedat, [Desolation](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Desolation) Demon, and [Whip of Erebos](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Whip+of+Erebos). Nakada, on the other hand, had a couple of tricks up his sleeve: [Brave the Elements](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Brave+the+Elements) and [Orzhov Charm](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Orzhov+Charm). Both players had the tools to destroy the other, but only one could be crowned Grand Prix Shizuoka champion.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/26/12/261204df9f3ee3c905e2423449011ff0e30b1a53b44bf3c513ffc57abd724eb2.jpg)  
*Shota Takao* 

### The Games

After a turn of trading creatures, Nakada amounted a quick army: [Soldier of the Pantheon](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Soldier+of+the+Pantheon), [Banisher Priest](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Banisher+Priest) and [Daring Skyjek](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Daring+Skyjek).

Takao created an army of his own with a pair of [Desolation](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Desolation) Demons. Unphased, Nakada used [Brave the Elements](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Brave+the+Elements) to halve Nakao's lifetotal, dropping him to 7.

[Whip of Erebos](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Whip+of+Erebos) came down prompting Nakada to sacrifice his [Mutavault](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mutavault) and use an [Orzhov Charm](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Orzhov+Charm) to protect against the demons. Nakao left a [Mutavault](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mutavault) untapped making Nakada unable to simply close the game out with another [Brave the Elements](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Brave+the+Elements).

On the following turn, Takao got greedy. When he neglected to use [Whip of Erebos](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Whip+of+Erebos)' ability in order to cast a pair of creatures, Nakada used [Brave the Elements](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Brave+the+Elements) to take the first game.

### Nakada 1 - 0 Takao

Despite mulliganing to 5, Nakada came out of the gates quickly with [Soldier of the Pantheon](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Soldier+of+the+Pantheon), [Precinct Captain](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Precinct+Captain) and [Xathrid Necromancer](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Xathrid+Necromancer).

![](https://i.ormos.uk/b7/52/b7520ce68b5a1c4ee7de03be00be9ac93049b30bdc2fddf3bfd2a8b9c0403ccf.jpg)  
*Ryo Nakada* 

Takao had some answers though, he used [Ultimate Price](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ultimate+Price) to remove the Soldier, [Detention Sphere](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Detention+Sphere) to exile the Necromancer, and [Glare of Heresy](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Glare+of+Heresy) to take out the Captain. Takao followed that up with an [Obzedat, Ghost Council](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Obzedat%2C+Ghost+Council).

Nakada found another [Soldier of the Pantheon](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Soldier+of+the+Pantheon) to keep Obzedat at bay, but Takao conitnued dropping big creatures in the form of a pair of [Desecration Demon](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Desecration+Demon)s.

After a turn of Demon beatdown, the writing was on the wall. Although Nakada was able to stay alive for another turn by sacrificing creatures, Takao's demons sent the match to a deciding game three.

### Nakada 1 - 1 Takao

![](https://i.ormos.uk/e7/0a/e70aa073e9152da1861b5c8cd46dffc165221d3fb1311bb87d14208f35a9d7f4.jpg)

Nakada had a blazingly fast start for the final game of the tournament: [Soldier of the Pantheon](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Soldier+of+the+Pantheon), [Xathrid Necromancer](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Xathrid+Necromancer), [Boros Elite](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Boros+Elite), and [Imposing Sovereign](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Imposing+Sovereign).

Takao lagged behind. While he was able to muster up an [Imposing Sovereign](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Imposing+Sovereign) of his own, the Necromancer made trading disadvantageous.

Nakada doubled down casting even more creatures. Knowing that even if Takao had a [Supreme Verdict](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Supreme+Verdict), [Xathrid Necromancer](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Xathrid+Necromancer) had his back, Nakada was fearless. Takao did what he could to attempt to prolong the inevitable, but after a couple of attack steps Ryo Nakada became Grand Prix Shizuoka Champion!

![](https://i.ormos.uk/61/02/61021e4ef4e5f91c802a023a68e1fa3fbfcb0d37632e2e04cfe86a7f83abc831.jpg)

### Congratulations to Ryo Nakada, Grand Prix Shizuoka Champion!

* * *

## Top 5 Cards of Grand Prix Shizuoka

*by Ben Swartz and Josh Bennett*

 

 

![Master of Waves](http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&name=Master+of+Waves)

 

### 5\. [Master of Waves](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Master+of+Waves)

Mono-Blue Devotion was a popular choice for players this weekend, and a successful one too. It was the most numerous Day 2 deck. Nearly a fifth of Sunday players were serving [Thassa, God of the Sea](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thassa%2C+God+of+the+Sea), and the [Master of Waves](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Master+of+Waves) is her favorite disciple. It gives the Mono-Blue deck an almost combo-deck feel, and it works extra hard against the red-based decks that were also out in force. Interestingly, Tomoharu Saito played it in the sideboard of his Blue-White Control deck, teaming up with [Frostburn Weird](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Frostburn+Weird) to give his slow deck a fast route to victory.

 

![Domestication](http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&name=Domestication)

 

### 4\. [Domestication](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Domestication)

You can't go far wrong with [Mind Control](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mind+Control), and this one was working both sides of the room, played in both the Blue-Based Control decks and the Mono-Blue Devotion decks. Especially powerful against aggressive red strategies (why yes, I would like a pet [Boros Reckoner](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Boros+Reckoner)), it was one of the reasons that none of the many red decks in Day 2 managed to crack the Top 8. Creatures are very much in vogue at the moment, so you're never short of targets. Finalist Shota Takao mentioned that his lone [Cartel Aristocrat](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cartel+Aristocrat) was added to the deck specifically to fight [Domestication](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Domestication).

 

![Pack Rat](http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&name=Pack+Rat)

 

### 3\. [Pack Rat](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Pack+Rat)

What can we say, it's a menace for a reason. The opening of [Thoughtseize](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thoughtseize) and [Pack Rat](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Pack+Rat) is just brutal, and both Mono-Black Devotion and the Black-White Midrange decks thrived this weekend because of it. Still, players came prepared for it, stocking up on [Ratchet Bomb](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ratchet+Bomb), [Last Breath](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Last+Breath), and [Detention Sphere](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Detention+Sphere). Interestingly, it also turns up in the sideboard of control decks like Shouta Yasooka's as a nasty surprise for players who skimp on removal in sideboarded games.

 

![Obzedat, Ghost Council](http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&name=Obzedat%2C+Ghost+Council)

 

### 2\. [Obzedat, Ghost Council](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Obzedat%2C+Ghost+Council)

[Blood Baron of Vizkopa](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Blood+Baron+of+Vizkopa) was the card everyone seemed to be turning to for help, but they paid a price in forgetting the head of the Church of Deals. The ghosts hit harder, dodge sorcery-speed removal, and can steal a game without ever hitting the Red Zone. They even play nice with [Whip of Erebos](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Whip+of+Erebos), coming back for good thanks to their own exile ability.

 

![Xathrid Necromancer](http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&name=Xathrid+Necromancer)

 

### 1\. [Xathrid Necromancer](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Xathrid+Necromancer)

The breakout card of the tournament, the Necromancer found a home both finalist's decks. It demands an immediate answer from removal, and makes combat a nightmare for the opponent. It also foils any plans that revolve around [Supreme Verdict](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Supreme+Verdict). In the semifinals, Shota Takao paired it with [Cartel Aristocrat](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cartel+Aristocrat) to suddenly double his army and defeat Shouta Yasooka. In the finals, however, the Necromancer had changed its allegiance. With its help, Ryo Nakada was free to summon a vast army, knowing that [Supreme Verdict](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154524/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Supreme+Verdict) couldn't save Takao, and so became the Grand Prix Shizuoka Champion.
